# Playtest ledger

**What this is.** Every item the operator has reported from a play test, in one place,
so that (a) nothing is lost between waves, (b) a repeat is visible as a repeat, and
(c) the operator has a reference to check, in a real game, whether a thing is
actually fixed.

**The rule that makes it useful: only the operator can mark an item verified.** The
tracker records what shipped and proves it from tests and commits, but a test that
passes is not a game that plays. The `Your check` column is yours: say `✅` or `❌`
after your next game and the tracker records it with the date and the build. An item
is not *done* until it has a `✅`. A `❌`, or the same thing reported again, reopens it
and counts as a regression if it had a `✅` before.

**Statuses**

| status | meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `open` | reported, not scheduled |
| `planned wNN` | in a wave file that has not landed |
| `shipped wNN` | on `main`, deployed, and proven from tests/commits by the tracker. **Not yet checked by you.** |
| `partly` | some of it shipped; the rest is named in the item |
| `reopened` | shipped, then reported again or marked ❌; the new report's ID is in the item |

**Builds.** A report is tagged with the build it was played on, so "it was already
fixed" can be answered from the version. Playtest 1 was before w29 closed, playtest 2
on `0.26.x` before w31, playtest 3 on `0.27.0` (w31).

**Asked** is how many times the operator has raised the same thing, counting across
lists. Three or more is a signal that the fix keeps missing what was meant.

**Not imported yet:** the 2026-09-28 lists (waves 25–28) live in the board's wave
sections, not here. Say the word and they come in.

## Check these in your next game

**Draft pods (2026-10-17, waves 70–72; ui 0.32.314 / server 0.32.209):** AF136 a pod of three: rounds, the bye, the board
strip from a watcher's seat. AF137 draft → Draft complete → build → play your built deck. AF138 a 1v1 room with sideboarding:
rematch, swap a card, see it in game 2; then bo3. Each row links its recorded run.

**Only what a person can judge**, and nothing a known bug will spoil. Updated 2026-10-07 after the tracker's batch and wave 54 landed and deployed
(deploy tree `4207ea4`: UI 0.32.43, server 0.32.19).

**From Playtest 10 and 11, please re-check:**

1. **Placement (Z8):** drop a land three columns right of your others — the gap stays; drop into a gap — it fills.
2. **Arrows (Z9, AA13):** Cryptbreaker targeting itself and two neighbours — three visible arrows, drawn over the cards.
3. **Mulligan (Z2, AA1):** mulligan to six — your own pop-up names the count, no "Waiting for…".
4. **Auto-pay (Z4, AA6):** cast a spell your lands pay for — no Pay Mana Cost pop-up at all.
5. **The other seat (Z5, AA3–AA5):** Bella's pointer at the mirrored spot; her held card back at hand-card size; a
   placed card flies from the back to its slot.
6. **Words (AA7–AA9):** End Turn with no yield pop-up; "Select creatures to attack."; "Resolve?" over a stack.
7. **The log (Z11, AA11):** no scrollbar beside the status block; a click outside or Escape closes it.
8. **Piles (AA12):** an untapped land behind a tapped one stays upright.
9. **The stack band (AA10):** cast with Fast Mode off — the row never flashes at the top.
10. **Deck boxes (Z1):** open the picker — every box the same size, long names end in an ellipsis.
11. **Attackers (Z6):** attack with Lightning Angel (vigilance) — it is outlined; Memnite turns and is outlined.
12. **Picks (W2):** mulligan to six — the card you picked looks picked, the others only offered.
13. **Blocks (Z7, L15, W14's blocker half):** block, see the arrow from your blocker to the attacker, click the blocker again to undo, block again, confirm.

14. **No pop-up the server answers (Z4 ×3, AB11, w57):** cast Bitterblossom with lands up — no cost pop-up, no "Resolve?"; End Turn with a creature out — their board never lights (AB9).
15. **The mulligan pick (Z2 ×3, AB2):** mulligan to six — one sentence, one button, OK greys until the count is met.

**Watch for:** a "Waiting for…" flash right after both keep (AB3, a client banner, not yet fixed); a card dragged out of hand stalling at the battlefield edge (AC1, fixed, intermittent before).

## Playtest 5 — 2026-10-03 (01:02 UTC), match `m-b81d965aa0fb`, two humans

**Played on a half-landed build:** the server was still `0.30.0`, but wave 35's Forge jar (lands
tappable) had been installed into production at 09:59 PDT, before w35's server code that handles the
newly-offered lands. So every land was clickable at priority with the old auto-pass logic. Auto-passing
was NOT broken wholesale (153 auto-passes in 89 min, the same rate as the game before the swap), but an
oddity at a specific stop may come from it (T3 is the candidate). Nothing from w35 was live otherwise.

| ID | item | asked | status | evidence | check it by | your check |

| T3 | an extra OK after declaring attackers that makes no sense | 2 (AF26) | **shipped `f7ad0b8`** (server 0.32.1, 2026-10-03) | Root cause in `apps/server/src/play/auto.ts`: `COMBAT_DECLARE_ATTACKERS` is in the default stop set, and `decideStop` honoured the stop set before the nothing-to-do rule, so the priority window right after the attack input was a second stop with `offered=[]`. Landed: J — one handoff path, one back builder, the dead arrow producers gone, one arrow builder, J12; S — one match transport contract in `@hadoku/shared`, one policy name, the dead yield path gone, the `arrangement` log row; T — the video helper, one mana table (AE20), the shared cast/grind helpers, LiveBoard/prompts knip to 0. | AE12 | "placing cards is very buggy still … (1) when i release godless shrine and then move to answer the question … from the opponents view that the phantom card is still attached. that's nonsense. (2) after the question is answered, the card correctly animates to the right spot but the phantom card is STILL attached to the cursor until i move the mouse again. please i beg you to get this right. we've already spent like 4 days on this" | 4 (= AA4, AB6, AD7, AE6) | shipped `71cb1f2` (the tracker's worker, 2026-10-08) | two mechanisms in `live/presence.ts`, both in the SENDER's words for its own card: (1) a PARKED card (let go, waiting on the question) was reported as `held`, so every pointer move drew the back at the pointer on the other screen AND abandoned the placement's gap wait (`draw`'s held branch called `stopWaiting`); (2) presence spoke only on pointer events, so when the card landed nothing was sent until the next mouse move. Now the wire names three states — `held` = lifted and following; `placing.at` on EVERY message while parked (not only the release); neither = gone — and a `MutationObserver` on the gesture's `data-cast-lift` mark speaks the moment the state changes. The receiver draws from that alone; a cleared message gives a point wait a 1.5 s grace (a cancel) and never touches a placement's gap. Proof `e2e/w62-parked.spec.ts` (two browsers, Godless Shrine): the back's every drawn position recorded on bob's screen from before the release; her pointer crosses ~700 px to the pop-up — the back does not move; she answers by a dispatched click (no pointer move) — the back is gone and the Shrine is on his board | drop a shockland, carry the cursor to the pop-up: the other screen's back stays at the release point; answer without moving the mouse: the back leaves as the card lands | ✅ 2026-10-09 ~04:10Z, from the review clips: "i went through most of the videos and they look pretty good to me. i think we can land it" |

## How the tracker keeps this

**The gate since 2026-10-08 23:40Z (the operator: "a recorded video of the thing working must be shown to
me before it passes"; earlier that night: "most things i ask for i ask for them 4-5 times before it
actually gets fixed"):**
- A reported item becomes a two-browser spec of the operator's EXACT sequence (pointer steps, both screens
  recorded) that is RED on main before any fix; the fix lands only with it green, in the same land.
- The spec's claw run is dispatched with `-f video=true`; the run keeps `alice.webm`/`bob.webm` of the
  sequence (`forge-e2e-videos-<attempt>-<shard>`). The tracker publishes both videos on a review page
  and writes the row as `landed <sha> — video: <link>`.
- Only the operator writes ✅ ("shipped" is theirs). A second report on a landed row is a REGRESSION row
  naming the commit that caused it (AE18, AE19 are the first two).
- No mechanism is named in a brief without a measurement; a lane's first commit measures.
- One owner per reported path across waves (presence / placement / arrows: wave 64's J), serial.


- **At every wave landing:** each shipped item moves to `shipped wNN` with the commit
  and the one-line "check it by", and the "next game" list at the top is rewritten
  from what just landed.
- **At every new list:** each item is checked against this ledger first. A repeat
  bumps `asked` on the original and gets a cross-reference, instead of becoming a
  fresh row that looks new.
- **Verification is yours.** The tracker never writes a `✅`.

| AF400 | (playtest 2026-10-26, item 4/5, Lands deck vs Reanimator `m-6eed2f853a79`) "elephant grass can't pay mana this was a soft-lock situation. maybe because there was a fetchland involved? is it true that we cannot crack fetchlands during declare attackers?" | 1 | open | log: `Elephant Grass\n\nPay Mana Cost: {G}` reached the seat. Rules answer: a fetchland is not a mana ability, so it cannot be cracked *inside* a payment (cumulative upkeep or the attack tax); it can be cracked with priority before (upkeep before the trigger resolves, beginning of combat) and after attackers are declared. The soft lock — a payment with no way to pay or decline — is the bug | | | |
